# Convoke Initiative Lifecycle & Backlog

**Created:** 2026-04-12
**Origin:** Party mode session — John (PM), Winston (Architect), Amalik
**Status:** Draft (Pass 2 — full backlog populated from retroactive review)

---

## Part 1: Lifecycle Process

The lifecycle process is defined in [`lifecycle-process-spec.md`](../../_bmad/bme/_enhance/workflows/initiatives-backlog/templates/lifecycle-process-spec.md) and nowhere else — intake (§1.1), the
qualifying gate (§1.2), the three lanes (§1.3), portfolio attachment (§1.4), pipeline stages (§1.5) and RICE
scoring (§1.6). **References to `§1.x` elsewhere in this file point there.**

It is deliberately not duplicated here. This file is the operational surface; the process it operates under is
defined once. Until 2026-08-26 these 182 lines were a byte-for-byte second copy — roughly 15% of a file already
too large for an agent to read in one pass, and a definition that could drift from its source without anything
noticing. Nothing operational read it: Triage and Review load the process from the spec file, not from here (T71).

## Part 2: Backlog

**Migration note:** Pass 2 reclassified all 88 items from `convoke-note-initiatives-backlog.md` into the three-lane model. Original IDs preserved for traceability. Items previously in "Completed" moved to §2.5. Items previously in "Exploration Candidates" moved to §2.1 (Intakes) — they never passed a qualifying gate.

| IN-63 | Governance ADR filename-collision on cross-day re-runs with identical stats — `adr-artifact-governance-convention-${date}.md` keys the filename on date, but identical `(renameCount, injectedCount, linksUpdated, scopeDirs, taxonomy)` on a different day produces two distinct ADR files (different filenames, byte-identical bodies apart from the `Date:` line). One logical governance decision ends up represented by two ADRs. Post-BUG-2 fix, content-identity idempotency works within a single day; cross-day identity isn't handled. | IN-64 | Add `windows-latest` to the CI matrix — I67 introduced `scripts/test-runner.js` to replace POSIX shell globs, but `.github/workflows/ci.yml` still runs only on `ubuntu-latest`. Without a Windows job, future regressions that break `cmd.exe` compatibility (e.g., someone reintroduces a shell-glob in an npm script) won't be caught by CI. Extend the `test` job's OS matrix to `[ubuntu-latest, windows-latest]` and validate `npm test`, `npm run test:integration`, `npm run test:p0`. Expect a small CI runtime increase (Windows runners are slower + add concurrency pressure); acceptable trade-off to lock in cross-platform. *(qualified 2026-08-31 → T106; claim re-verified against `.github/workflows/ci.yml` first — 11 `ubuntu-latest`, zero Windows runners.)* | I67 Winston handoff (2026-04-20) | 2026-04-20 | Winston |

Path (d) was deferred for I67 to avoid bundling an engines-bump with a scripts-fix, but Convoke 4.0 is the right vehicle: Node 18 is EOL (2025-04-30), Node 20 is in maintenance, Node 22 is current LTS, and major versions are the canonical moment to raise engines. | IN-185 *(qualified → T55, 2026-08-24)* | **No workflow mode can close or correct a backlog row — the format spec defines a transition the tooling cannot perform.** `backlog-format-spec.md` §"Moving Items Between Sections" specifies *"Any → §2.5 Completed (shipped): Move row to Completed sub-table with shipping date"* and *"Never delete a row outright."* No step file implements it. **[R] Review** rescores RICE only and is forbidden to change status in three places (`step-r-02-rescore.md:117`, `step-r-03-update.md:34`) and forbidden to touch §2.5 (`step-r-03-update.md:35`, `:32` forbids adding/removing rows). **[T] Triage** ingests *new* findings into §2.1. **[C] Create** bootstraps a file. So a row that ships can only be flipped by hand-editing outside the workflow — which is why BUG-8 sat `Open` for 6 days after `cc685063`, and why A47/I103/I104 sat shipped-but-open for ~3 months (closed 2026-08-14). The recurring diagnosis has been operator inattention; the actual cause is a missing mode. Suggested shape: a `[K] Close/Correct` mode — lane row → §2.5 with shipping date, status flips, Change Log entry, and a validation that a closing row cites its shipping evidence. → T55 | Backlog correction session (John PM) | 2026-08-15 | John |

### 2.3 Fast Lane (Quick Wins + Spikes)

Items qualified as not needing the full initiative pipeline. **Sorted by RICE score, descending** — mechanically re-sorted 2026-08-15, after the lane was found to be sorted only *within* appended triage batches rather than across the lane.

Ordering contract, so the next re-sort is reproducible rather than a judgement call:

1. **Live rows first**, score descending. Ties keep their prior relative order.
2. **Untriaged rows next** — those carrying `?` for R/I/C/E and `—` for score. They have no sort key, so they cannot participate; they belong in §2.1 Intakes and are parked here until triaged.
3. **Closed rows do not remain in a lane at all** — anything `Done`, `Closed`, `Shipped`, `Superseded`, `Rescoped`, `Absorbed` or `Invalid`, or marked ✅, **moves to §2.5 in the same edit that closes it**. Closing a row and leaving it in the lane is an incomplete edit, not a deferred chore — see §"Closing a Row" in [`backlog-format-spec.md`](../../_bmad/bme/_enhance/workflows/initiatives-backlog/templates/backlog-format-spec.md). *(Corrected 2026-08-25: this inline copy still read "They stay in the lane for provenance" after the 2026-08-24 amendment changed the spec, so the file instructed the opposite of what CI now enforces.)*
4. **Supersession pairs move as one unit.** Where a marker row is immediately followed by the original text under the same ID (D14, I131), the pair sorts on the original's score and stays adjacent. See I150.

**Read the Status column, not just the position.** A row's rank is only as honest as its status cell: D14 sat at score 2.1 in live priority order while its own marker row said "superseded 2026-08-13", because the status cell was never flipped — the same failure that left A47, I103 and I104 heading this lane for three months as already-shipped work.

This is the fourth and the first whose victim was the check rather than the product. **Why review cannot see it.** Three full adversarial review rounds — nine subagent passes, 83 findings, every one of them executing the code — ran on a machine where `node_modules` existed, so the dependency resolved every time. The set-equality and mutation disciplines that caught everything else are blind to it. Only the runner has the property that matters. **Blast radius beyond this row.** The invariant also holds *"only while `package.json` has no `prepack`/`prepare` script"*, per the same comment; nothing checks that either. `dist-2-6` is about to modify the install path this job exercises. **Shape of a fix, to be decided rather than assumed:** assert in CI that every script `try-fresh-install.sh` invokes resolves its requires against `node:` builtins and the installed tree only — cheaply done by running the job's scripts once from a checkout with `node_modules` removed, which is what reproduced it here in seconds. Same end state follows a mid-upload cancellation (see T48). **Consequence: run status is not evidence of registry state in either direction**, which undercuts every rollback procedure that reads the job result. Compounding it, the state is unrecoverable by re-tagging -- once the version exists, every subsequent push of that tag fails at `npm publish` permanently, and npm's unpublish window is 72 hours and refuses outright once there are dependents. **Fix:** a post-publish step that reads `npm view <pkg> dist-tags` plus `dist.attestations` and asserts they match what the job intended, so the job's own exit code becomes trustworthy. **ABSORBED 2026-08-23 — T35's residual, the out-of-band twin of the same gap.** `dist-1-7` closed T35's token vector at the registry but cannot close the interactive one: no npm setting blocks a 2FA human from hand-publishing, so detection replaces prevention. Reproduced only under an artificial near-instant exit (40/40 transcripts complete under realistic timing) and deliberately not fixed — draining tee means closing and reopening stdout, and `/dev/tty` does not exist on a CI runner. Resolved 2026-08-14 → the `_warnings` row became I151 and intake IN-111 was repointed. That instance is the proof the mechanism fails silently: it survived every read of this file, including several deliberate audits. The other three surfaced only because a diff or a human happened to catch them — I131 (PF1 methodology vs Python/`uv` preflight, resolved → I132); I130 (PF1 placeholder-guard vs upstream-name gate, resolved → I143 by a parallel session); I143 itself (upstream-name gate vs T25 deferrals, resolved → I149 by this session). Every instance is the same mechanism: a session derives the next ID from the backlog as it stood when that session started, while another session is allocating from the same space. Two of the three were caught only because a human or a diff happened to surface them — a collision is silent by construction, since both rows render fine and `grep '^\| I143 \|'` returning two lines is something nobody runs. **Consequence:** cross-references become ambiguous (`related: I143` now means one of two things depending on when it was written), and the staleness-preflight's dependency check reads the wrong row. **Options:** (a) a `next-id` counter line in the backlog that allocation must increment; (b) a `bmad-enhance-initiatives-backlog` allocation step that re-reads and re-derives at write time; (c) session-scoped prefixes. **A duplicate-ID assertion is worth adding regardless of which** — it would have caught all four at the moment they were introduced. **One nuance it must handle:** this backlog deliberately uses ADJACENT same-ID pairs for supersession (a marker row immediately followed by the original text — see D14 and I131). A naive uniqueness check false-positives on those. Flag only NON-adjacent duplicates; that distinction cleanly separates the two deliberate pairs from all four real collisions. | 5 | 2 | 90% | 2 | 4.5 | convoke | Qualified | found-by: 2026-08-14 T25 R2 preflight; instances: I131, I130, I143 |

Chokepoint catches the common path; CI catches the bypass."* **Verified 2026-08-26: neither assertion is implemented.** `grep -inE "review.record\|dev agent record\|senior developer review" .github/workflows/ci.yml` → no hits; no job asserts a test-touch or opt-out; no script under `scripts/audit/` mentions review. The sentence is a `documentation-claims-must-be-derived` violation inside the file that defines that rule. **Measured cost, same session:** `code-review-convergence` triggers Round 2 on *any* HIGH in Round 1. Round 1 of `gen-1-1` found a HIGH; Round 2 was skipped with the reasoning *"the HIGH is fixed"* written into `7b957dbf` and pushed. 132 lines shipped unreviewed. Rounds 2 and 3 — run only because the **operator asked** — found **4 more HIGH**, the worst being a fix that deleted 41 of 53 manifest rows while reporting "other modules preserved". **Why a rule is not the fix:** the rule already said all of this and was violated by the agent that had read it. `feedback_verification_basis` records the identical skip from a prior session, also caught by the operator. Two instances, one written lesson, zero prevention. **Build the gate or downgrade the sentence to a proposal** — leaving it in the indicative is worse than silence, because it tells the next reader a bypass would be caught. **Suggested shape:** a `scripts/audit/review-record-check.js` asserting (a) any story at `done` has a Dev Agent Record naming each round and its outcome, (b) a commit touching `scripts/**/*.js` touches a test or carries `[no-test]` with a reason. Prove it red before trusting it. **ATTEMPT 1 BUILT AND REVERTED 2026-08-26 — the row's own shape was measured and rejected first, then the replacement failed too.** **Measured before building, and this part stands:** every naive form of this gate is theatre. "A done story has a Dev Agent Record" passes **134/134**, INCLUDING the story that skipped a round. "A commit mentions a Round" passes **both** real skips, because both did. "A `scripts/**/*.js` commit touches a test" fails **52% of the 46 such commits in the last 300** and is about test discipline, not review discipline. **So the row as filed cannot be built as specified.** **Attempt 1 asserted CLAIM CONSISTENCY instead:** if a commit message reports Round 1 with any HIGH, it must report a Round 2 outcome, because `code-review-convergence` triggers Round 2 on any HIGH. It caught both real failures (`7b957dbf`, `5439c8b4`) with 2 hits in 300 commits and zero false positives — and Round 1 killed it anyway. **Why it died: it discriminates on punctuation, not meaning.** Deleting ONE character from the real `5439c8b4` message — the colon in `Rounds: 1 complete` — makes the unmodified offending commit PASS. So does `no HIGH outstanding` (the exact phrase that offender used), `one HIGH` instead of `1 HIGH`, `Round 2 not needed` instead of `not triggered`, and a period before the count. A reviewer enumerated **19 of 19 realistic evasions that pass**. An author editing their message until CI goes green learns the gate is a wording puzzle. **Two structural faults beyond the regexes:** the check cannot distinguish an author skipping a round from a commit *describing* someone skipping one, so every post-mortem or archive commit self-blocks — including the T77 commit itself, in a job inside `publish.needs`; and an empty commit range prints PASS and exits 0, the vacuous-gate shape this project has been burned by twice. **And the part that should carry the most weight: I wrote unreproducible numbers into `project-context.md`, inside the bullet that cites `documentation-claims-must-be-derived`.** Claimed 188 done-stories / 132 with a file / 56 without / 17 qualifying / 171 predating; actual **264 / 134 / 130 / 9 / 125**. The "17" conflated *files carrying `baseline_commit`* with *done stories carrying it*. The old sentence asserted a gate that did not exist; the replacement asserted measurements that do not reproduce — the same defect one level more sophisticated. `project-context.md` is reverted to the honest "the backstop does not exist". **The reviewer's recommendation, and the design decision this now needs:** stop parsing prose. Require a structured, greppable review trailer (git trailers, e.g. `Review-Rounds: 1:3H 2:0H`) that cannot be defeated by wording and cannot be tripped by a commit that merely quotes one. That is a convention change touching `commit-preparation`, it is forward-only because no historical commit carries it, and it is an operator decision rather than an implementation. **Precedent says do not patch the regexes a second time:** BUG-10's guard was withdrawn for hand-rolling a lexer in regex, and the backlog parser is recorded as "fenced in, not correct" after three rounds. Attempt 1's full diff is preserved in the session scratchpad. **Effort raised 2 -> 3, score 4.05 -> 2.7:** the blocker is a convention decision, not code. | 3 | 3 | 90% | 3 | 2.7 | convoke | Backlog | found-by: gen-epic-1 retro 2026-08-26; operator-selected as the epic's primary action item |

| T37 | 2026-08-15 | **`drift-snapshot.js` table escaper lets a lone CR through, and its HTML entities may render literally inside the code spans it wraps every cell in.** `scripts/audit/drift-snapshot.js:127` encodes pipe and backtick as HTML entities (`&#124;` and `&#96;`) — a strategy deliberately different from the shared backslash escapers, and its R2-M1 comment gives a genuinely good reason: backslash escapes are not processed inside CommonMark code spans, and every cell here is backticked. **Correctly left unconverged by BUG-12** — forcing it onto the shared escaper would be a regression. Two gaps found while making that call. (1) **Lone CR is unhandled and reachable.** Verified by execution: the escaper returns a lone CR unchanged where the shared escaper collapses it to a space, and `normalize` at `:73` strips only CRLF — a string containing a bare CR splits into **1** line, so the CR survives into a rendered row. CommonMark treats a bare CR as a line ending, so it breaks the row exactly as a newline would; that is the same class the shared escaper already handles. (2) **Entities inside a code span are literal text** (CommonMark §6.1), so an encoded pipe plausibly renders as a visible entity rather than a pipe character. Spec-derived, **not** renderer-verified — no markdown library is installed in this repo, so confirm against a real GitHub render before acting on (2). **Fix:** add CR/LF collapsing to the escaper regardless of (2); re-check (2) first. **Correction to the record:** BUG-12 working notes called this approach *strictly better* than the shared escapers. The unbounded-growth half of this bug IS fixed (measured 858 → 1209 → 1209, stable); only the false audit trail remains. **(3) A refusal to write is indistinguishable from a no-op.** When the salvage copy fails, `mergeTaxonomy` returns `{created:false, merged:false, promoted:[]}` plus a `skipped` field, but `refresh-installation.js` branches only on `created`/`merged` — so an operator whose `_bmad/_config` is read-only gets a clean-looking update forever while the taxonomy is never merged. | I142 | 2026-08-14 | **`readManifest` claims RFC-4180 handling but line-splits naively, so a multi-line quoted CSV field silently DROPS rows.** Found 2026-08-14 by R1 review of I139. `scripts/portability/manifest-csv.js::readManifest` and the I139 seeding block both do `readFileSync(...).split('\n')`, which cuts a row containing an embedded newline (e.g. a quoted multi-line `description`) into two garbled lines; the path column then misaligns and the row is **dropped with no error and no warning** even when its content is present. Reviewer reproduced it: a 3-row manifest with one multi-line field yielded `dataLines: 3, kept: 1` where 2 rows should have parsed and both target files existed. **NOT currently triggered** — verified against the real 106-row manifest, which contains zero multi-line fields (`readManifest` parses exactly 106 rows, matching `wc -l`). **Why it matters anyway:** the module's own docstring claims RFC-4180 compliance, so the next person to add a description with a newline in it gets a silently shorter manifest and a skill that cannot be exported, with nothing pointing at the cause. **Fix:** make `readManifest` parse quoted fields across line boundaries (or state plainly in the docstring that multi-line fields are unsupported and reject them loudly). Silence is the defect. | 4 | 2 | 85% | 3 | 2.3 | convoke | Qualified | found-by: 2026-08-14 R1 review of I139; related: I139, `manifest-csv.js` |
| I128 | 2026-08-11 | **`spawnSync('git', …)` cannot launch Windows `git.cmd`/`git.bat` shims — dirty-tree check silently fails open on Windows.** Found 2026-08-11 (Blind Hunter; reasoned risk, **not executed** — this is a darwin-only environment). The 2026-08-11 hardening replaced `execSync` (which went through `cmd.exe` and resolved PATHEXT) with bare `spawnSync('git', …)`, which does not apply PATHEXT. On a Windows install where git is a `.cmd` shim, `res.error` is set → `runGitDiff` returns `''` → `{dirty:false}` → uncommitted work is overwritten without warning. `package.json` declares no `os` restriction and the module has zero `win32` handling, so Windows is nominally supported. **Fix options:** probe with `where git` / try `git.exe`, or surface the spawn failure instead of folding it into the fail-open path — note the fail-open itself is by design and predates this (see `checkDirtyTree`'s catch), so the fix is about *distinguishing* "git absent" from "tree clean". Bundle with **I127**, which touches the same function. | 3 | 3 | 50% | 2 | 2.3 | convoke | Qualified | found-by: 2026-08-11 code review; unverified-on-windows; bundles-with: I127 |

| T75 | 2026-08-26 | **`agent-manifest.csv` recovery is classified by header literal, not by parse — so a corrupt manifest duplicates every agent, and the obvious fix deletes 41 upstream rows.** Three review rounds on story `gen-1-1` each attempted this and each was wrong in a different way; the diagnosis is that all three were written against the code's model of the file rather than against the file. **Current shipped behaviour (`7b957dbf`):** `isV610Header` is a two-state test — anything not v6.1.0 is *assumed* legacy. A manifest whose header line was dropped, or that carries a merge-conflict marker, is parsed as legacy; the 12-column rows then fail the legacy `bme` test (index 8 is `principles`, not `bme`) so they are preserved as foreign while fresh rows are appended. **Measured: 53 rows → 64, every bme agent duplicated**, under a junk header. **Do NOT fix this by adding an `unusable` state that regenerates from scratch** — that was attempted 2026-08-26 and reviewed out: it drops `preservedRows`, so the same input went **53 rows → 12, deleting all 41 upstream rows** (18 bmm, 12 cis, 4 wds, 3 bmb, 2 tea, 2 core) while still returning *"other modules preserved"*, and `validateManifest` still reported pass because it only checks that Convoke's own IDs are present. Degraded data became deleted data. **Also rejected: a positive `legacy` predicate built from `buildAgentRowLegacy`'s field list.** It matches none of the three headers that have ever existed here — 62 manifest blobs across 21 commits yield `"agent_id",...` (1×), the current 12-column header (13×), and a 10-column `name,...,path` header (48×). A real legacy manifest would have been salvaged away: a working file renamed out from under the user. **The known-correct approach**, per `refresh-installation.js:518-522`, is to classify by **parse + required-column presence** rather than by header literal, carry preserved rows through the recovery path by parsing the salvaged body, copy-before-write (a failed salvage currently lets the regeneration clobber the original), use a unique salvage name (`fs.rename` overwrites a same-version `.corrupt-` file), and `changes.push` so the operator learns a file was set aside. **Note the 10-column header case is pre-existing and survives all three rounds:** it passes `startsWith('name,')` so 12-column rows are written under a 10-column header and the filter reads the wrong index. Full analysis in the gen-1-1 Round 3 record. **ATTEMPT 4 ATTEMPTED AND REJECTED 2026-08-26 — do not re-derive it either.** The approach: when the header is unusable, parse EVERY line (including line 0, which is a data row when the header was lost) and keep whatever the module does not own, classifying with a predicate shared between writer and reader. It genuinely fixed the reported case — 53 rows restored exactly, 41 upstream preserved, idempotent, all 19 historical blobs lossless — **and was still wrong in two new ways plus one it did not touch.** (a) **`isUsableHeader` was purely negative** (`contains "path"` && `!contains "_bmad/"`), so `# TODO: fix the path below` was accepted as a header and recovery silently skipped. A header predicate must assert something POSITIVE — column count, known column names — not merely fail to look like a data row. (b) **Recovery hardcoded `V610_HEADER`**, so a legacy 10-column manifest got a 12-column header over 10-field rows; readers zip header-to-row by index, giving `module = "_bmad/bmm/agents/pm.md"` and `path = undefined`. It broke the module's own documented contract that the schema never changes underneath a consumer. Schema must be INFERRED from the surviving rows' field count. (c) **The 10-column header case was untouched**: it passes both `isUsableHeader` and `isV610Header`, takes the normal branch, and the index-9 misfilter duplicates every Vortex agent — stable at 2x across three runs, never healing. That is 48 of 62 historical blobs. **The architecture question this exposed, which is the real blocker and needs an operator decision:** attempt 4 shared one row-classifier between the generator and `validateManifest`, on T76 Round 1's finding that a reader classifying differently from the writer disagrees with it permanently. Round 1 of this attempt found the converse — **sharing guarantees they agree but does not make them right**: `validateManifest` returned clean on both new failure modes, and the attempt's own duplicate-detection test counted with the writer's classifier and reported zero duplicates on `0d2c15fc`'s manifest, where every Vortex path appears twice. That is `project-context.md`'s own "a check that shares its classifier with the thing it checks cannot detect a classification bug", reproduced inside the fix for it. **Decide the reader/writer coupling before attempting 5.** **Latent, fix alongside:** `refresh-installation.js:663` logs the hardcoded `MANIFEST_CHANGE_MESSAGE` while pushing the generator's return value, so any new return message makes verbose output contradict the changes array. Attempt 4's full diff is preserved in the session scratchpad; it is reconstructible from this description. **Effort raised 2 -> 3 and score 3.0 -> 2.0**: four attempts have now failed, and the blocker is a design decision rather than an implementation. | 2 | 3 | 100% | 3 | 2.0 | convoke | Backlog | deferred-from: gen-1-1 Round 3 (no Round 4 per code-review-convergence); sibling: T54 |

| I136 | 2026-08-14 | **Three known, deliberately-unclosed gaps in the `checkDirtyTree` write guard.** Logged 2026-08-14 from R2/R3 adversarial review of I127 so they are not carried only in code comments. **(1) `GIT_WORK_TREE` / `GIT_INDEX_FILE` neutralisation is UNVERIFIED.** `gitEnv()` clears all three inherited git vars but only `GIT_DIR` has a discriminating test. Two attempts at one were written and both proved VACUOUS under mutation — leaking those vars makes `rev-parse` report cwd as outside the work tree, so the guard fails open to the same observable as the correct answer. Deleted rather than kept as decorative coverage; the gap is documented in the test file. **(2) `hasGitAncestor` may disagree with git across a mount point** — git refuses to cross filesystem boundaries during repo discovery by default, an `fs.existsSync` walk does not. Fails CLOSED if they diverge (a spurious refusal, never a silent overwrite), so it is safe but could produce a confusing "refusing to write" in an unusual container layout. Reasoned, NOT executed — the reviewer could not construct a second filesystem in the sandbox. **(3) The returned `diff` field is not a diff** — it is the file's own path, from `--name-only`/`ls-files` output. The boolean `dirty` is computed correctly and independently, so this is a contract/naming defect, not a safety one, but 5 call sites surface it to operators under a name implying it shows what changed. **Fix (1) or accept it explicitly; (2) needs a real mount to test; (3) is a rename plus a real `git diff` call.** | 3 | 2 | 60% | 2 | 1.8 | convoke | Qualified | found-by: 2026-08-14 R2/R3 review of I127; related: I127 |

Measured 2026-08-26 against the shipped `validateManifest`. **T76 deliberately did not fix this, and a validator cannot.** Those rows are written by BMAD's own installer; Convoke has no source of truth for how many a given project should have, so there is nothing to compare against at validation time. **The detectable version is a before/after comparison inside one refresh:** `refreshInstallation` already reads the existing manifest to compute `preservedRows`; asserting that the count of preserved rows does not DROP across a regeneration is cheap, needs no external truth, and would have caught the gen-1-1 Round 3 defect that deleted 41 rows while reporting *"other modules preserved"*. **Also residue from T76, all deliberate:** duplicates and an unrecognisable header now **warn rather than fail** — correct, because failing at `migration-runner.js:146` rolls the update back to the same file and wedges the consumer, but it means those signals reach a human only during an actual `convoke-update`, printed in gray beside routine info. **And `validateManifest` is not in any publish gate:** `convoke-doctor` never calls `validator.js` (0 references), so nothing checks the manifest on the `fresh-install` path. Wiring it there is a separate, cheap decision — but note `convoke-doctor.js:641` prints only `check.info`, so warnings would be swallowed. **Sequencing:** independent of T74; complementary to T75 (which owns the generator-side repair). **ATTEMPT 1 BUILT AND REVERTED 2026-08-24.** **The proposal in this row was corrected first.** It said the fix is "a before/after comparison inside one refresh". T74's Round 2 corrected that for the CI case — the loss lands IN HEAD, so there is no *before*; a static floor is what catches it there. The runtime before/after IS available inside the generator, and that is what attempt 1 built: `assertNoRowsDestroyed(inputRows, outputRows)` called immediately before the write, throwing if a non-blank input row is absent from the output and carries no `bme` field. **It works, measured: 365 executions across every manifest shape in this repository's history plus 27 malformed ones produced ZERO false throws**, and it catches the gen-1-1 Round 3 defect byte-for-byte with the file left untouched. It was still reverted, for four reasons. **(a) It provably cannot fire on the branch every real manifest takes.** The v6.1.0 filter drops a row iff `parsed[9] === 'bme'`, which makes `parseCSVRow(row).includes('bme')` necessarily true, so `destroyed` is always empty there. It is live only on the legacy branch. **(b) Where it IS live, it is wrong.** The legacy filter classifies with a quoted-group regex over `fields[8]`; the guard re-derived ownership with `parseCSVRow`. A legacy row with whitespace adjacent to the quoted module field is dropped by one and flagged by the other — **it throws on a legitimate row**, and the throw rolls back the consumer's update. Two predicates that must agree, derived independently: the exact defect T76 Round 1 found, reproduced. **The fix is to share the filter's own drop decision rather than re-derive it.** **(c) It can be switched off with every test green.** A lint-clean one-line `if (options.conserve !== false)` disables it: 45/45 pass, lint 0. That is the THIRD failed attempt at asserting this wiring — `indexOf`, then a statement-position regex, both defeated by a guarded call. **A source-text assertion cannot close it; the wiring test must be behavioural.** **(d) "Throwing is safe because it rolls back" was generalised from one caller.** `migration-runner.js:146` holds a backup; `install-vortex-agents.js:181` and `install-gyre-agents.js:140` do not, so a throw there strands a half-installed tree. The error message also asserted *"This is a bug in the generator, not in your manifest"* — false: the one reachable production trigger IS the user's manifest. **Also from Round 1, unfixed:** the guard shares `existingRaw.split('\n').slice(1)` with the code it checks, so a lost header (line 0 consumed as the header) and a CR-only file (guard inspects zero rows) both reconcile cleanly while rows are destroyed — "a check that shares its derivation with the thing it checks cannot detect a derivation bug". And no conservation test used a real manifest; the highest-value regression test ("every historical manifest regenerates without throwing") does not exist and is ~10 lines. Attempt 1's diff is preserved in the session scratchpad. **Effort raised 2 -> 3, score 2.7 -> 1.8**: the guard is worth having as a regression tripwire on the preservation filter, but it is not the runtime data guarantee this row assumed, and the framing needs deciding before code. | 2 | 2 | 90% | 2 | 1.8 | convoke | Backlog | deferred-from: T76 (partial close); sibling: T74, T75 |

| BUG-19 | **Two non-blocking warnings fired on a healthy install and an expert validator read them as failure.** Part (a) — the label asserting the opposite of its own finding — shipped 2026-08-28 in `21ae3105`. Part (b) split to **BUG-20** and remains open: whether an *expected* absence should warn at all. FR18 closes here via story `dist-2-5`, which got the fix wrong twice before measurement corrected it. **First:** the story specified shipping the package's registry in `files[]` and copying it — but its sole row is an `auto-scan` row for `bmad-register-skill`, a skill in no user project, so copying reports `[stale:skill-gone]`, stale by construction. **Second:** generating the registry from a scan of the project looked right — it is what the doctor's `fix:` line tells the *operator* to run — but the installer is not the operator: on any project with existing custom BMM-dependent skills it stamped THEIR rows with the reserved `auto-scan` marker, which broke `convoke-register-skill` (`exit 1`, duplicate-triple) and permanently suppressed the doctor's `unregistered-custom-skill` detection, reporting `✓ registry consistent` over an ungoverned tree. Caught by Round 1's Blind Hunter; **the original evidence could not see it because it was measured on a project with no custom skills.** Shipped fix: seed an EMPTY registry — the schema, never a row — published by an **inline atomic create-if-absent** (`writeFileSync` to a sibling temp → `linkSync`, which fails `EEXIST` atomically → `unlinkSync`). A torn registry here would be permanent and unhealable even by the documented fix command, and `linkSync`'s `EEXIST` also closes a measured 6–10 ms window in which a concurrent `convoke-register-skill` commit was destroyed. **Three review rounds and two rejected mechanisms preceded this:** a Round 1 delegation to `_atomicWrite` was reverted in Round 2 because delegating hid the write from `install-scope-check.js`, re-greening a CI gate by invisibility; and the `lstat` guard's original rationale — *a dangling symlink makes the installer write outside the project* — is **false** of the shipped code, since `linkSync` never follows a symlink. `lstat` is a fast path; the surviving hazard it addresses is replacing an operator's symlink. Empty projects report **All 28 checks passed**; projects with unregistered skills keep the correct warning and a working path to green. | 2026-09-01 | 5.7 | convoke |

| BUG-8 | **Migration rollback could not restore the files the migration rewrites.** `getFilesToBackup()` backed up only 4 `_bmad/bme/_vortex/*` paths, while the 3.3→4.0 migration rewrites the 19 `SKILL.md` inventory targets + `bmad-init/SKILL.md` (zero overlap), and `restoreBackup` iterated that static list rather than a manifest — so rewritten skills were unrecoverable while the operator was told "restored." **Absorbed audit #3** (state-file `migration-state-4.0.yaml` surviving rollback — same safety-net hole, handled via the delete-on-rollback class). Reproduced 2026-06-28. **✅ Fixed 2026-08-09 in `cc685063`; verified against source 2026-08-15 — both halves, not just the one that surfaced first.** Migrations now declare `getBackupManifest()`; `scripts/update/lib/migration-runner.js:88-105` collects the write-set and passes it to `createBackup`; `scripts/update/lib/backup-manager.js:179-185` restores manifest-driven, with a legacy static-list fallback for backups predating `backup_entries`. A backup scope that cannot be determined **hard-aborts before any mutation** rather than degrading-and-continuing (Round-2 review decision — nothing has changed yet, so no rollback is needed). Review hardening added a path-containment gate rejecting empty, absolute, `..`-escaping, and project-root-equal `relPath`s. **The row nonetheless stayed `Open` for six days and survived the 2026-08-14 recount** — not inattention: no workflow mode can close a row (IN-185). Its live text also called itself a "release-blocker for 4.0" while scoring 2.7 and sitting ninth in the lane; the label and the score contradicted each other and neither was reconciled. Story: [bug-high2-migration-backup-rollback-scope.md](../implementation-artifacts/bug-high2-migration-backup-rollback-scope.md). | BUG-2 | ADR generation idempotent no-op commit — `scripts/migrate-artifacts.js:398-404` no longer downgrades a legitimate re-run to a misleading `"ADR generation failed"` warning. Root cause: on re-run with byte-identical ADR content (same date + same stats), `git add` stages nothing and `git commit` errors with `"nothing to commit"`; the outer catch previously caught that and warned with exit 0. Fix (Winston path b — detect-and-skip no-op): after `git add`, probe `git diff --cached --quiet` with `err.status === 1` discrimination; on no-staged-diff log `"ADR already current: <filename> (no changes to commit)"`, on staged changes run the existing commit + log `"ADR generated: <filename>"`. Rejected path a (`--allow-empty` — pollutes git history) and path c (monotonic counter in ADR content — defeats idempotency). Aligns with existing check-then-act pattern in `supersedePreviousADR` (`scripts/lib/artifact-utils.js:2104`). Outer try/catch preserved so real ADR failures still degrade gracefully. T4 test suite updated: `ALLOWED_WARNING_PATTERNS` tightened to only the legitimate first-run "Previous ADR not found" warning (the two byte-identical-ADR allow-list patterns removed — their appearance now signals a genuine regression); positive `assert.match(result.stdout, /ADR already current/)` in test 3 locks in the no-op branch. 2 rounds of review applied 4 patches (0 HIGH): Round 1 discriminated `err.status === 1` from other git errors, added positive stdout assertion; Round 2 corrected comment inaccuracies (git's `--quiet` contract is "non-0, non-1 = error", not ">1"; rethrown errors surface underlying messages but outer catch still prefixes "ADR generation failed:"). Convergence at Round 2 — comment-only patches, no structural changes → no Round 3 eligible. Full gate: `npm test` 1178/1178, T4 3/3. 2 items deferred: broad `git add _bmad-output/planning-artifacts/` can sweep unrelated staged changes (pre-existing; failure mode shifted from loud-error to silent-sweep post-fix), TOCTOU window between `git add`/`git diff`/`git commit` on concurrent invocations. 1 follow-up logged: **IN-63** (cross-day ADR filename collision — same stats on a different day produces two ADR files with byte-identical bodies apart from `**Date:**`). | 2026-04-20 | 5.4 | convoke |

| I64 | `runScript` helper (`tests/helpers.js:153`) return-shape hardened for `execFile` timeouts. Pre-I64: timeouts left `err.code = null`, which leaked out as `exitCode: null` and caused opaque `null !== 0` diagnostics in any CLI-integration test hitting a timeout (pre-existing bug surfaced by T4 Round 1 warning-guard). Post-I64: `exitCode` is always a finite integer (coerces null-but-errored to 1); two new fields `timedOut` (true iff execFile killed child for exceeding `timeout`) and `signal` (kill signal e.g. `'SIGTERM'`, else null) surface distinct timeout semantics so callers get actionable failure messages. Return shape documented in JSDoc contract. New `tests/unit/helpers.test.js` (4 tests) locks the contract: clean exit → exitCode 0 / timedOut false / signal null; non-zero exit → exitCode propagated / timedOut false; 200ms-timeout on hang script → timedOut true / signal SIGTERM / exitCode is finite integer ≠ 0; shape-test asserts all 5 keys present. Full gate: 1177 unit (+4) / 85 integration. First application of A27 staleness pre-flight — all 4 checks GREEN. | 2026-04-19 | 4.5 | convoke |
| T4 | Migration idempotency CLI test — new `tests/integration/migrate-artifacts-idempotency.test.js` exercises `convoke-migrate --apply --force` against a real git-initialized fixture. Final: 3 tests covering first-run rename + commits, second-run no-op (exit 0 + zero new commits), third-run resumes on added ungoverned file. Convergence at Round 1 (no production code touched). 5 items deferred: migration meta-artifact re-flagging (original finding), **ADR byte-identical bug** (surfaced by Round 1 warning-guard — real bug), `runScript` null exitCode on timeout (pre-existing helper bug), 5-commit scan boundary, missing zero-ungoverned baseline test. | 2026-04-19 | 2.4 | convoke |

| BUG-17 | **`convoke-doctor` reported module-version skew that `convoke-update` could not act on.** Doctor checked every `_bmad/bme/*/config.yaml`; `getCurrentVersion()` read only `_vortex`, so an install whose Vortex was current but whose siblings were behind reported skew forever — the gate returned `up-to-date` and exited before `refreshInstallation`, which has stamped every module since `ag-7-1`, was ever reached. **Closed by making the gate enumerate and compare the same way doctor does** — `scripts/lib/bme-modules.js`, shared `discoverModules` plus `detectRepairableSkew`, consulted by `assessUpdate` after the up-to-date verdict. `getCurrentVersion()` is unchanged (its scalar feeds the migration registry, which keys off Vortex's history) and **`convoke-doctor` is byte-identical to before** — it already reported the skew correctly. **Scope was cut deliberately after three review rounds.** Attempts to also classify every version shape and decide what doctor should advise failed three times, each round's remedies introducing defects the next round found; the widened design was reverted rather than patched a fourth time. What that leaves open is filed with reproductions: **T114** (a source checkout repairs nothing, so the advice is still wrong there), **T115** (four gate branches refuse before skew is consulted), **T116** (a numeric `_vortex` version crashes `convoke-update`), plus **BUG-18** and **T38**. | 2026-09-02 | 4.5 | convoke |

A CI job-timeout kill would be ignored and a hung suite would report success. The residual leak on a group-external kill is accepted and documented in-code; Ctrl-C is unaffected. ### P21 — Convoke Operator Covenant

**Stage:** Epic 1 ✓ (shipped 2026-04-19) | Epic 2 — Story 2.1 startable, Story 2.3 gated (see T86) | **Portfolio:** convoke | **RICE:** 9.0

**Origin:** Party mode session 2026-04-18 (Sally, John, Loom Master, Winston, Paige, Bob, Dr. Quinn, Victor). Renamed from "Convoke Experience Contract" during that session. Qualified 2026-04-18 by Winston (per §1.2 shortcut rule).

**Planning artifacts (Epic 1 complete):**
- Candidate doc `loom-note-backlog-candidate-experience-contract.md` (pre-rename, archival)
- Epic spec `convoke-epic-operator-covenant.md` — `status: ready-for-sprint-planning`, two epics defined (Epic 1: Define; Epic 2: Adopt + Publish)
- Covenant reference doc `convoke-covenant-operator.md` (axiom + 7 Operator Rights + scar stories + JTBD + audience guidance)
- Compliance Checklist `convoke-spec-covenant-compliance-checklist.md` (OC-R0..OC-R7 + 3-layer audit scope + 6 status values + reproducibility gate via Selection Discipline: Skill / Step / Cell)
- Baseline audit `convoke-report-operator-covenant-audit-2026-04-18.md` (calibration cases: Migration + Portfolio; methodology locked)
- Vortex audit `convoke-report-operator-covenant-audit-vortex-2026-04-19.md` (extended methodology)
- Epic 1 retrospective `oc-epic-1-retro-2026-04-19.md` (7 action items)
- Stories `oc-1-1`..`oc-1-5` all shipped 2026-04-19

**Missing (to reach Epic 2 Ready):** *(corrected 2026-08-27 — the previous text named blockers that were never Story 2.1's.)* **Story 2.1 needs nothing** — it is independent per epic :202 and `oc-2-1` is specced; its story context needs refreshing from 3 cells (A24) to 11 (A24 + A26 cluster, both A10-cleared). **Story 2.2** blocked on P25 *(parked 2026-08-25)*. **Story 2.3** blocked on the Publication Gate — see T86 for the corrected reading of both arms.

**Scope hint (two-epic architecture):**

1. **Epic 1 — Define the Covenant (✓ shipped 2026-04-19):** Two-artifact structure (Covenant reference + Compliance Checklist). Self-compliance gated (Covenant document passes its own Checklist before shipping). Audit methodology locked with calibration cases (Migration + Portfolio as known violators). Adoption surface coverage in contributor-facing docs (bme module README, agent-builder skill intro, CLAUDE.md rule `covenant-compliance-for-convoke-skills`).

2. **Epic 2 — Adopt and Publish (deferred):**
   - Story 2.1: Retrofit bottleneck rights — A5 trigger rule locked (T1 concentrated < 70% cell at N ≥ 3; T2 systemic < 75% overall or any Right < 70%). v1 baseline doesn't fire either trigger — Epic 2 correctly deferred pending IN-12 Vortex re-audit.
   - Story 2.2: Wire Compliance Checklist into Loom Add Skill workflow (Phase 3 dependency — blocked on external P12/Loom work).
   - Story 2.3: Publication Gate — publish Covenant as external positioning material. Blocked on Story 2.1 completing bottleneck-rights retrofits (don't publish a covenant you can't keep).

**Strategic positioning (Blue Ocean wedge):** Competitors (CrewAI, LangGraph, AutoGen, Agentic Mesh) compete on capability density. Convoke competes on operator experience as a first-class architectural concern. The Covenant + Checklist are internal quality gates that *become external launch material* once the methodology earns credibility through audit. Impact scored I:2 pre-publication (internal compliance value); re-score trigger `I:2 → I:3` fires when Story 2.3 publishes.

**Dependencies and triggers:**
- Epic 2 Story 2.2 blocked on external Phase 3 Loom Add Skill workflow (P12/Loom team factory — separate initiative).
- P24 (relocate Covenant + Checklist out of `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/`) is a soft prerequisite for Story 2.3 Publication — can't publish pointing at a workflow-output path.
- IN-12 (Vortex re-audit) pending — if re-audit fires T1/T2 trigger, Epic 2 Story 2.1 retrofit scope expands.

**Axiom:** "The operator is the resolver."

**JTBD:** Operators don't hire Convoke to produce output. They hire Convoke to feel in control of decisions they don't fully understand.

### ILE-1 — Initiative Lifecycle Engine

**Stage:** Ready for Sprint | **Portfolio:** helm | **RICE:** 2.7

**Origin:** Party mode session 2026-04-12 (John, Winston, Amalik). Qualified 2026-04-15 by John+Winston (per §1.2 shortcut rule).

**Planning artifacts (complete):** Brief (B) + distillate; PRD (P); pre-Architecture IR; Architecture (A) — 8 ADRs + 24 patterns + complete project tree + validation; Epics (E) — 6 epics × 67 stories, 63/63 FR coverage; post-Architecture+Epics IR (IR✓) — `convoke-report-implementation-readiness-initiative-lifecycle-engine-post-epics.md` shipped 2026-04-19, verdict **FULL READY** (0 critical issues, 0 major issues, 0 blocking concerns across all 5 validation categories).

**Missing (to reach Ready):** none. **Ready for Sprint 0 execution.**

**Scope hint (three workstreams):**

1. **Rework existing skills as views on a shared model.**
   - `bmad-enhance-initiatives-backlog` becomes the **write surface** (lifecycle-aware, lane-aware) — already reworked in 2026-04-15 session as v2.0.0; data model still implicit
   - `bmad-portfolio-status` becomes the **read surface** (cross-initiative dashboard, pipeline completeness, WIP signals)
   - `bmad-migrate-artifacts` feeds the shared model (artifact creation auto-advances pipeline stage)
   - All three need a **shared data layer** (structured source + generated markdown views)

2. **Enhance with new capabilities:**
   - Kanban view (columns = lifecycle stages, rows = initiatives, WIP limits)
   - Backlog-portfolio integration (creating a PRD auto-advances stage)
   - Pipeline completeness dashboard (live "what artifacts exist?" computed from disk)

3. **Reactive behaviors layer:**
   - New initiative logged → orphan intake scan suggests attachment
   - Artifact created → pipeline stage auto-advances
   - Sprint closes → staleness detector flags broken file references
   - RICE rescore requested → pulls current context (recent ships, adjacent changes)
   - Epic completed → close absorbed items + unblock dependent items

**Why helm (portfolio):** This is strategic governance tooling. Per §1.4 taxonomy, helm covers strategic governance — this is its flagship feature. Helm doesn't exist as a built team yet, but initiatives can attach to portfolio items that aren't yet scaffolded (precedent: P9 attached to forge before Forge team exists).

**Why Initiative (lane):** Multi-module (3 skills + new shared layer), architectural (shared data model + reactive behaviors), user-facing (kanban + new commands), substantial scope (three workstreams). All §1.3 Initiative triggers fire.

**Risk note:** Portfolio attachment (helm) is a current-best-fit, not a final answer. If WS3 planning surfaces a better home (e.g., a new "governance" portfolio item), revisit per §1.4 growth rule.

**Scope precedents already absorbed (do not duplicate in pipeline):**
- WS1 (process rules + restructure) and WS2 (retroactive review) — already executed as spikes on 2026-04-15 (Pass 1 and Pass 2 of the lifecycle backlog)
- The skill rework of `bmad-enhance-initiatives-backlog` v2.0.0 — already shipped 2026-04-15, but only the write surface; the shared data model and integration with the other two skills remain

## Change Log

| Date | Change |
|------|--------|
| 2026-09-02 | **BUG-17 shipped in TWO commits, not one — the second fixed a recurrence of BUG-17 inside BUG-17's own fix, and the record above does not say so.** `90ca7de0` landed the reduced change; `a0539c83` landed the findings of a narrow review pass over `scripts/lib/bme-modules.js`, the one file no earlier round had seen in its shipped form (it was rewritten after Round 3, so the three rounds reviewed larger, different code). **F1, the finding worth keeping:** the reduction read `mod.config.version` where `convoke-doctor` reads `version \|\| installed_version`, so a module declaring only `installed_version` was visible to doctor and invisible to the gate — doctor reports skew, `convoke-update` says up-to-date, and a refresh would have repaired it. **That is BUG-17's exact symptom, recreated inside its fix**, and an earlier revision of the story had named the hazard verbatim in an acceptance criterion before the rewrite discarded the AC and the code together. Reproduced by execution, fixed to exact parity, pinned by a regression test. Latent rather than live: nothing in the repo writes that key, and `convoke-doctor` holds its only read. **The transferable lesson is not the bug — it is that rewriting from scratch loses the defences embedded in what you replace, and a diff cannot show you what a rewrite silently drops.** Also fixed in the same commit: a swallowed `require` failure that made every module look unmanaged, so a broken installer module turned a skewed tree into `✓ Already up to date!` and exit 0 — a silent green, now a loud throw; a header comment claiming a single-sourcing the commit deliberately did not do (doctor keeps its own enumeration and comparator, because leaving it byte-identical was a requirement — the two copies are pinned by tests and review, not by construction); and an Update Plan labelling a `divergent` module `Behind:` when equal precedence with a different string is not behind. **Corrected gate figures superseding the entry above:** `npm test` 1981/1980/1 skip, `npm run test:integration` 124/124, lint clean, `backlog-integrity.js` exit 0. | 2026-08-24 | **T66 shipped — the gate that reported success while failing.** A suite-level `after()` hook that throws does not increment `fail` and does not set a non-zero exit code, so `npm test` and CI both reported green; `afterEach` behaves correctly, which is why CI run 32115225495 went red while this class stayed silent. It is how a real teardown regression survived three verifications during T61. Fixed by running a second TAP reporter to a side file and treating any `not ok` as failure when the child exits 0. **Two measurements changed the design mid-build:** node:test sets `NODE_TEST_CONTEXT` in every child, and a nested `node --test` inheriting it exits 0 even on real failures — the first red run was measuring the harness, not the runner, and the test now scrubs it; and the default reporter varies across the CI matrix (Node 20 piped emits TAP, Node 25 spec), so 'preserve the default' was not a coherent goal and `spec` is now pinned uniformly. Four tests added, red before green. Run against the full suite it found **zero** hidden failures — the existing suite is genuinely clean, which was not knowable before. |
| 2026-08-24 | **T61 shipped as a ratchet, and its own estimate disproved.** The row assumed ~250 sites at effort 2. Measured: **221** recursive removals inside `after`/`afterEach` across **61** files, plus 245 more elsewhere — and only ~21 of those 61 contain any subprocess, so 40 have no writer that could race them. A blanket sweep was also riskier than it read: `removeTempDir` refuses paths outside `os.tmpdir()`, so each site needs judgement, not a codemod. Landed instead as a **ratchet**: `no-restricted-syntax` on `tests/**` bans bare recursive removal in teardown, the 20 subprocess-bearing files are converted (47 sites), and the 40 writer-free files are grandfathered with the reason recorded as *no writer* — so adding a subprocess to one of them means converting it and deleting it from the list. The allowlist is the tracker and only shrinks; no follow-up row filed, because a row to convert 40 files with no known risk is the churn this decision avoided. Two manual calls: `registry-writer-injection.test.js` deletes individual marker files non-recursively and got an inline exception, not a grandfather entry; `dual-distribution-parity.test.js` lost nullity guards and a `.catch(() => {})` that would have swallowed the survivor listing. Rule mutation-tested (violation -> exit 1, restored -> exit 0); `npm test` 1678, `test:integration` 120/120, lint exit 0. |

| 2026-08-11 | **Two remote-code-execution holes closed in `_bmad/bme/_team-factory/lib/writers/registry-writer.js` (Winston; found by adversarial code review).** Triggered by external PR #4, which correctly reported a **shell-injection** hole: the old escaping handled only `\` and `'`, so a path containing `"`, `$` or a backtick escaped the quoting and reached the shell at three `execSync` sites. Verified by execution and fixed by moving all three to `spawnSync` with argv arrays. PR closed rather than merged — its version dropped `path.resolve` in `verifyRequire`, and `node -e "require('foo/bar.js')"` without `./` is a *package* specifier (verified: exit 1 MODULE_NOT_FOUND vs exit 0 resolved) — with the finding credited in a PR comment. PR #5 closed as not-applicable (targets a `.py.bak` file that is never executed, not published, and not collected by CI). **The review then found something worse than the reported bug, in the same file:** (1) **`icon` was the single field interpolated raw** while its nine siblings all used `escapeSingleQuotes` — a spec with `icon: "X', zz: require('fs').writeFileSync(…), yy: '"` produces syntactically valid JS, **passes `validateSyntax` with status 0**, and is persisted; (2) **`team_name` was interpolated into a `//` comment**, so a newline escaped the comment — verified end-to-end returning `success:true, errors:[]` with the payload **written permanently into `agent-registry.js`**, which is require()d by refresh-installation, validator, convoke-doctor, the installer, `index.js` and migration-runner, so it re-executes on every subsequent Convoke operation. Both confirmed by execution before fixing. **Fixes:** `icon` escaped; new `sanitizeForComment` applied at **both** teamName comment sites; `derivePrefix` constrained to `[A-Z0-9_]`; `res.error.stderr` preserved so timeout/ENOBUFS keep `execSync`'s diagnostics; `status === null` + signal handled so an OOM/cgroup kill no longer rolls back a correctly written file; a false docstring corrected (`path.resolve` was **newly added** to `validateSyntax`, not "retained"). **New regression suite** `tests/team-factory/registry-writer-injection.test.js` (4 tests) — written because all 165 team-factory tests passed *identically* before and after the vulnerable code, i.e. the suite could not detect either hole. **It immediately earned its place: the first teamName fix was incomplete** — a second interpolation at `:260` (`// Derived lists for ${teamName}`) still leaked the payload, and the test caught it; the remaining sites were then enumerated mechanically rather than eyeballed. Gates: lint 0 · unit **1511/0** · integration 0. **Three pre-existing items logged, not fixed here:** **I126** (`_bmad/` excluded from ESLint — the systemic reason a raw interpolation sat unnoticed beside nine escaped ones), **I127** (`checkDirtyTree` false-clean on relative paths → overwrites uncommitted work; pre-existing and faithfully preserved by the hardening), **I128** (Windows PATHEXT / `git.cmd`). |
